Use this file for gaps discovered during project work that do not clearly belong
to the active project's own gap tracker. This is a repo-level surfacing and
routing file for cross-project, orphaned, or out-of-current-scope gaps.

When a living project is created or refreshed, check this file before creating
new gap rows. Import only the gaps that genuinely belong to the project after
critical scope review.

| GG-364 | open | correctness_gap | River scene to WebGPU (2026-09-30), Remy: "can we move this to webGPU?" | Two three 0.172 WebGPU faults the river worked around touch other WebGPU pages. (1) The scene fog is applied twice to every lit node material (the WGSL writes the fogged color, then fogs it again). (2) The hemisphere light node weighs the sky by the plain vertex normal and dots that view-space normal with a world direction, so its light turns with the camera; the ocean viewer's buoys (`oceanExtras/buoys.ts`) use a HemisphereLight on WebGPU. The river fixes both only for itself: the fog density over sqrt(2), and `RiverHemisphereLight` with a node registered on its own renderer. | `docs/architecture/domains/world3d-river-water.md`, "The renderer (WebGPU)" and "Frame cost"; `riverReachScene.ts` (`RiverHemisphereLight`, the fog note); a gray sphere under the river's lights drew 5 to 7 % darker than in WebGL before the fix | A lit WebGPU scene with fog is twice as hazy as its numbers say, and a hemisphere-lit object brightens or darkens as the camera turns: wrong looks that no error shows. | three.js on WebGPU (ocean viewer, any page with fog or a HemisphereLight) | route | Ocean gauntlet lead (buoys), the river water domain (the shared fix) | Move the river's light class and node to a shared module and use it on every WebGPU page with a HemisphereLight; give fog on WebGPU one owner; or upgrade three past the fix. | A buoy under a hemisphere light at two camera headings: the same hull brightness. |
| GG-363 | open | tech_debt | River scene to WebGPU (2026-09-30), Remy: "can we move this to webGPU?" | The river's WebGPU port rests on workarounds for three 0.172: the fog density over sqrt(2); the river's hemisphere light node; the leaf cards' alpha-to-coverage ramp and cast-shadow discard; the shadow prime drawn twice and for the mirror's camera; the plant copies culled through `instanceCount` with `count` at the capacity; the read-back helper's row padding; a placeholder uv on every base texture node; `positionGeometry` in the quads; a sample with the sampler off for a load. A three upgrade that fixes a fault can break the frame silently (for example, a single fog pass at d over sqrt(2) under-hazes by half). | `docs/architecture/domains/world3d-river-water.md`, "The renderer (WebGPU)" and "Frame cost" (the list, with the evidence of each) | The river's look depends on three's own faults; an upgrade changes it with no error. | River water / three.js upgrades | log | River water domain | On a three upgrade, check each workaround against the WGSL and the frames, and remove the ones three fixed. | After an upgrade, `shootPort.mjs` against the frames of the version before: the same numbers as the port's table. |

| GG-340 | open | correctness_gap | Ocean gauntlet, skip stones round 1 (2026-09-29); narrowed by round 2 (2026-09-29) | Round 2 found and fixed two causes of the drift, neither of them a rule that holds the tilt. Measured with npx tsx tools/_p3e6c8.mts (min over the cycle of &#124;LeftHandEnd world x&#124; minus hw + 0.044*H*bulk): run clearance is +3.2 to +4.5 cm on human m1, tabaxi m7, minotaur m6, orc m4 and warforged m8, but -0.2 cm on gnome f5. Each dirty file was copied to .agent/scratch/gg223/base/*.wc, its HEAD blob to *.head, then swapped in, tested, and restored byte-exact (cmp verified, all files locked under GG-223-bisect). Command: npx vitest run <files> --pool=threads --testTimeout=60000. No golden was regenerated. 1) roofStyle.golden > "keep-seed 7: identical bones, distinct roof + dress" (expected 2 to be 3) CULPRIT: src/systems/worldforge/town/architectureStyle.ts, the keep hunk (single file, sufficient and necessary). BEHAVIOR: adds "if (buildingType === 'keep') roofForm = 'flat'" to BOTH resolveArchitectureVariant and resolveStyle, plus hard-coded keep wallColor #a6a091, roofColor #626b70, trimColor #8d887c, facadePattern 'plain', and keepConstruction() forcing kitId 'keep-ashlar' / dressed-stone / slate. Pinning one roof form makes solveRoof return the same roof for two of the three style contexts, so the distinct-roof count drops 3 -> 2. CALL: intended design (deliberate defensive-masonry keep with a roof terrace) that regresses the test's style-variety invariant. Keeps no longer vary their roof by culture/climate/wealth. Needs a human decision: exempt 'keep' from the all-distinct roof assertion, or let keeps keep form variety. PROOF: swapping ONLY architectureStyle.ts to HEAD turns this test green while manor-bones and the roster golden stay red. 2) roofStyle.golden > "manor-seed7-bones" snapshot CULPRIT: three hunks jointly - src/systems/worldforge/interior/footprint.ts (TYPE_CONFIG size table), interior/partition.ts (ROOM_CAP + targetRooms divisor), interior/program.ts (isCorridorShape rewrite + kitchen/hall program). BEHAVIOR: footprint roughly doubles every building's cell footprint (manor mainW [8,12]->[12,16], mainH [6,9]->[10,13]); partition lowers per-type room caps (cottage 5->4, tavern 9->7, townhouse 6->5, inn 10->8) and raises the per-room area budget from total/12 to total/20 (~300 -> ~500 sq ft per target room); program replaces the straight-1-cell-run corridor test with a "no 2x2 habitable block" test, so bent five-foot strips now count as corridors. All three land inside bones (footprintCells, widthFt/depthFt, floors). CALL: intended design - a coordinated character-scale interiors change, each hunk carrying a matching rationale comment. Not a regression; but the pinned snapshot is the anchor the test says to regenerate with -u only for a deliberate geometry change, so it needs human re-approval before -u. PROOF: reverting {footprint, partition, program} to HEAD -> green. Any two of the three -> still red. doors.ts is not required. 3) generateTownRoster > "matches the frozen roster golden" CULPRIT: interior/footprint.ts + interior/program.ts (minimal set; partition.ts and doors.ts are NOT needed). BEHAVIOR: larger footprints plus the new corridor/kitchen/hall program yield more habitable rooms per plot, so the tiny mixed-use town generates 5 residents instead of 3 and reshuffles home plots (id 2 moves from plot 11 to plot 10; two extra residents Name-1056 / Name-9527 appear). CALL: intended downstream consequence of the same character-scale change, not an independent bug. The frozen roster golden needs human re-approval. PROOF: reverting {footprint, program} to HEAD -> green. 4) generateLocal > "pins terrain hash and feature counts" CULPRIT: NONE of the uncommitted edits. Swapping ALL 53 dirty tracked source files under src/systems/worldforge + src/utils to HEAD leaves this test red with identical numbers: featureCount 5670 -> 12636, materialHash 3952163437 -> 416382634, treeCount unchanged at 1620. BEHAVIOR: the golden's snapshot file last changed in commit df3adb7ec (2026-08-04), while its import closure took COMMITTED changes through 2026-08-19 - erosion/atlasErosionBake.ts, erosion/rockHardness.ts, region/regionTerrainField.ts, region/regionCompositeField.ts, region/generateRegion.ts, forests/forestTunables.ts, fmg/climate.ts, local/generateLocal.ts. Material classification shifted, so non-tree feature placement (rocks/bushes) more than doubled while the tree pass was unaffected. CALL: stale golden from committed drift, not an in-flight hunk. It belongs to whoever owns the erosion/region work, not to the character-scale branch. PROOF: node .agent/scratch/gg223/group.mjs local <all 53 dirty files> -> same failure, same numbers. CLEARED OF SUSPICION (in a closure, dirty, but behavior-neutral or unimplicated): - src/utils/random/seededRandom.ts - the diff is a dependency-header comment line only, zero behavior. - interior/blueprintTypes.ts - adds optional "skirts?: RoofPlane[]", type only. - interior/roofPlan.ts (+249 lines, roof skirts) and interior/doors.ts (kitchen door penalty) - neither is required to flip any of the four goldens. TREE STATE: unchanged. 47 modified + 19 untracked under src/systems/worldforge and src/utils/random before and after, every swapped file cmp-verified against its .wc copy. ## Status Vocabulary

| Status | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `untriaged` | Recorded for surfacing; no owning project has accepted it yet. |
| `candidate` | May belong to a known project, but needs critical scope review. |
| `imported` | Accepted into a project gap tracker; destination is linked. |
| `routed` | Sent to another existing subsystem tracker; destination is linked. |
| `declined` | Reviewed and intentionally not accepted; rationale is recorded. |
| `done` | Resolved with completion evidence linked or summarized. |

## Routing Rules

- Add gaps here when they are cross-project, orphaned, or outside the active
  project's current scope.
- Do not add gaps here just because they are inconvenient. If the active project
  cannot honestly complete without the gap, it belongs in the project tracker or
  project `GAPS.md`.
- When a project imports a global gap, copy the actionable context into that
  project's `GAPS.md`, then mark the global row `imported` and link the
  destination. Preserve the global row as routing history.
- When the gap clearly belongs to another established subsystem, mark the row
  `routed` and link that subsystem's tracker.
- When scope review rejects a gap, mark it `declined` with a concise reason.
- **Only change a row's Status for gaps inside YOUR active project's scope**
  (import/decline/route those). If a row belongs to a *different* project, leave
  its Status `untriaged` for the owner to claim â€" do NOT mark it
  `declined`/`routed`/`imported` on the owner's behalf. You may fill or correct
  the `Suspected owner/project` column so future out-of-score agents can skip it
  in one glance (a hint, not a status claim). A gap leaves the active triage pool
  only when its **owner** imports/declines/routes it. Truly orphaned,
  no-clear-owner gaps stay `untriaged` and should be escalated for a human
  routing decision rather than force-statused.

## Physical object registry for combat/world targeting - 2026-05-31

Source project: Structured Spell Execution (docs/tasks/spell-system-overhaul).

Problem:
- Spell targeting now has a minimal runtime object validation envelope, but there is no shared combat/world source of positioned targetable physical objects.
- Existing battle-map decorations are visual/terrain obstacle metadata, not spell-targetable object entities.
- Existing loot generation returns unpositioned item results, not battle-map object candidates.

Needed system:
- A physical object registry or adapter that can expose positioned object candidates with stable ids, names, positions, size, weight, magical status, worn/carried/fixed state, and enough map context for line-of-sight/range checks.

Why global:
- This is not only spell execution. It also touches battle-map interaction, loot placement, object inspection, inventory/world item state, and environmental manipulation.

Spell-side dependency:
- TargetResolver.isValidObjectTarget can validate supplied candidates once such a registry exists.

2026-06-12 local progress:
- Spell-side object targeting can now consume generated-map fixed objects and explicitly positioned loose Item inputs through ObjectTargetRegistry.
- The cross-system gap remains open because src/services/lootService.ts and inventory/world item systems still return or hold unpositioned items; a real producer must decide when an item is on the battle map before it becomes a target candidate.

(status at cells[1]) â€" every fallback card shows its metadata shifted one column left (e.g. confidence displays the Evidence text). Affects display only; per-project schema fields override when present. Fix the index map or the table header, in one place, for all projects. |

| GG-139 | untriaged | feature_coverage_gap | Understory layer verification (agora-de43, 2026-09-09) | A forest floor still renders as bare ground even though every vegetation layer above it works. Two independent ceilings cause it. (1) The understory feature streams are priced per 10,000 sq ft, and the densest biome in the table asks for `bushDensity 1.6 x FERN_MULT 3.6` = one fern per ~161 sq m; the fern geometry is 326 triangles, so raising the multiplier enough to matter costs triangles faster than it buys cover. (2) `buildGrassField` only emits where the terrain vertex color reads green, and a forest floor under canopy is painted brown, so the grass layer contributes nothing exactly where cover is most needed. Neither is a defect in the understory layer, which is placed, batched and tuned correctly; the missing piece is a separate cheap high-count cover tier (flat litter/low fronds at single-digit triangles, near-camera radius, conformed and lifted a few cm off the surface against z-fighting). | Measured live at `?phase=world3d&ground=1&dcell=785` (2026-09-09): 4,039 understory instances across the 5x5-chunk window, of which 2,242 ferns over ~409,600 sq m = one fern per ~183 sq m. Layer on/off pair at eye level in the densest stand changes 6.0% of the floor band (`.agent/scratch/combat-proofs/understory.png` and `understory-layer-off.png`). Density table: `src/systems/worldforge/local/generateLocal.ts` BIOME_PROFILES; multipliers `src/systems/worldforge/forests/forestTunables.ts` FERN_MULT/SAPLING_MULT/LOG_MULT; green-only gate `src/systems/worldforge/vegetation/grassField.ts` buildGrassField; renderer `src/components/World3D/vegetation/UnderstoryField.tsx`. | The beautification wave treats the forest floor as solved because the understory feature shipped, and the plan-map now records that layer as done. Anyone reading that will not find out that the floor still reads empty in play, and the next agent asked to "fill the forest floor" will re-derive this measurement from scratch or, worse, close the gap by raising a multiplier that cannot close it. | World 3D vegetation / beautification wave (planmap `world-props`) | untriaged | none | Design a near-camera ground-cover tier rather than tuning the existing multipliers: a flat litter/frond patch of under ~10 triangles, scattered from chunk noise the way grass is (not from the per-feature id stream, which cannot carry six-figure counts), gated on canopy or on the forest-floor material instead of on green vertex color, with its own chunk radius so the instance count stays bounded. Cost the CPU-side matrix build before committing - `UnderstoryField` composes every instance matrix on the main thread on each streaming change. | At eye level in a dense stand, the layer on/off pair changes a materially larger share of the floor band than the 6.0% measured on 2026-09-09, with frame triangles and draw calls reported alongside it and no z-fighting against the terrain. |
| GG-140 | untriaged | correctness_gap | Travel mechanics verification (agora-c8c4, 2026-09-09) | `SeededRandom` is a Lehmer generator seeded as `seed % 2147483647`, so its FIRST draw off a SMALL seed is very close to the minimum: seeds 1..9 every one of them roll a natural 1 on `nextInt(1, 21)`. Anything that creates a fresh SeededRandom per event from a small derived number therefore gets a rigged first roll rather than a random one. MapPane seeds the committed-trip navigation check with `(worldSeed ?? 0) + destCellId * 6271 + 29` and the trip-event roll with `(worldSeed ?? 0) + destCellId * 9973 + 41`, which are large for a real world but small for a dev world seeded 0 with a low cell id - that party is ALWAYS lost and always rolls a 1 on its trip event. | Reproduced 2026-09-09: `new SeededRandom(s).nextInt(1,21)` returns 1 for s = 1..9 (and 20 for s = 0, which wraps). Drawing 400 checks from ONE shared stream instead gives the expected distribution (372/400 lost at Survival -5 vs 77/400 at +10, DC 15) - see `.agent/scratch/combat-proofs/travel-mechanics-live.png`. Generator: `src/utils/random/seededRandom.ts`; travel seeding: `src/components/MapPane.tsx` (deriveNavDrift and rollTripEvent call sites). | Every seeded one-shot roll in the repo inherits this. A low-seed dev world silently produces worst-case outcomes, which reads as "the get-lost rule is too harsh" rather than as a PRNG artifact, and any balance tuning done against a dev world is tuning against a rigged die. | Core RNG utility (`src/utils/random`), consumed by travel, world-gen and encounter code | untriaged | none | Decide between hashing the seed before use (e.g. splitmix/xorshift finalizer on the constructor argument) and warming the stream by discarding the first draw. Hashing is the safer fix but changes every existing seeded sequence, so it needs an explicit determinism-break decision - `seededRandom.ts` is flagged CRITICAL CORE SYSTEM and world generation reproducibility depends on it. Do not change it as a side effect of a travel task. | A table of first draws for seeds 0..64 shows a flat distribution across 1..20, and the world-gen golden fixtures are re-recorded (or the change is shown to leave them byte-identical). |
| GG-141 | untriaged | feature_coverage_gap | Travel mechanics verification (agora-c8c4, 2026-09-09) | Forced-march exhaustion does not STACK. App applies exhaustion as the party-wide condition string `exhaustion` via `SET_PARTY_CONDITION`, and that reducer case is deliberately idempotent (a member already carrying the condition is untouched), so a party that forced-marches five days running is exactly as exhausted as one that marched once. 5e exhaustion is a 1-6 level track where level 6 is death; travel can currently only ever reach level 1. The travel-speed penalty added by this task reads a level (`partyExhaustionLevel` already parses a numbered form), so the missing piece is a numeric level in state plus a rest path that steps it down. | Proven 2026-09-09 by `src/systems/travel/__tests__/travelMechanicsVerification.test.ts` ("failed saves wear the party down..."): after the condition is applied the party level reads 1, and a second application leaves it at 1. Reducer: `src/state/reducers/characterReducer.ts` SET_PARTY_CONDITION; helper: `src/systems/travel/forcedMarch.ts` partyExhaustionLevel. | The forced-march rule loses its teeth exactly where it should bite hardest - a long overland push has the same cost as one late evening - and the death-at-level-6 consequence is unreachable, so the survival pressure the travel system is meant to create never arrives. | Travel / party condition state (planmap `maritime-remainder`, `travel` campaign) | untriaged | none | Carry exhaustion as a level rather than a flag: either a numeric field on the character or the numbered condition form `exhaustion_N` that `partyExhaustionLevel` already reads, with a matching decrement on a long rest. Reuse `calculateExhaustionEffects` (`src/utils/combat/physicsUtils.ts`) for the d20 and speed penalties instead of writing a second table; note that combat still does not apply it either (TODO #1313 in `src/utils/combat/combatUtils.ts`). | Two forced marches in a row leave the party at level 2 with a measurably slower route time than one march, a long rest steps it back down, and the condition chip shows the level. |
